{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We resolve the planar Sobolev endpoint of Carleson's convergence problem: for initial data in $`H^{1/3}(\\mathbb R^2)`$, the Schrödinger evolution converges almost everywhere to the initial data as $`t\\downarrow0`$. The evolution is defined by taking the Gaussian regularization limit first; on one full-measure spatial set, this limit exists for every $`0\\lt t\\lt 1`$, and the resulting evolution is continuous at <i>t</i> = 0.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the planar Sobolev endpoint of Carleson's convergence problem: for initial data in $`H^{1/3}(\mathbb R^2)`$, the Schrödinger evolution converges almost everywhere to the initial data as $`t\downarrow0`$. The evolution is defined by taking the Gaussian regularization limit first; on one full-measure spatial set, this limit exists for every $`0\lt t\lt 1`$, and the resulting evolution is continuous at <i>t</i> = 0.

- **claim-planar-endpoint-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $f\in H^{1/3}(\mathbb R^2)$ there is a set $E_f\subset\mathbb R^2$ of full Lebesgue measure such that, for each $x\in E_f$, the finite limit $$u_f(x,t)=\lim_{a\downarrow0}U_af(x,t)$$ exists for every real $0<t<1$, and $$\lim_{t\downarrow0}u_f(x,t)=f^*(x).$$ In fact $u_f(x,\cdot)$ extends continuously to $[0,1)$ with value $f^*(x)$ at zero, and the Gaussian limit is uniform over $0<t<1$.
  - Explanation: Almost-everywhere convergence of the Schrödinger evolution at the endpoint regularity, with the Gaussian limit taken first and the result continuous at time zero.
- **claim-exact-packet-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-packets)): Let $s\geq1$, let $v$ have frequency support in a fixed bounded set, and consider $\|t\|\leq Cs^2$. There is a decomposition $v=\sum_{\omega,n}a_{\omega,n}v_{\omega,n}$, with $\omega\in s^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^2$ in a fixed bounded enlargement and $b_n=sn$, $n\in\mathbb{Z}^2$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \sum_{\omega,n}\|a_{\omega,n}\|^2&\leq C\\|v\\|_2^2, \label{eq:prelim-packet-energy}\\ \left\\|\sum_{(\omega,n)\in\mathcal I} a_{\omega,n}v_{\omega,n}\right\\|_2^2 &\leq C\sum_{(\omega,n)\in\mathcal I}\|a_{\omega,n}\|^2 \quad\text{for every subfamily }\mathcal I, \label{eq:prelim-subseries-energy}\\ \|Ev_{\omega,n}(x,t)\|&\leq C_Ns^{-1} \left(1+\frac{\|x-b_n-2t\omega\|}{s}\right)^{-N} \quad(N\geq1). \label{eq:prelim-packet-decay} \end{aligned}$$ The constants are independent of the number of terms retained. The phase-space label counts are bounded, and every packet has frequency support in an $O(s^{-1})$ neighborhood of $\omega$.* *Fix $0<\eta<1$. On a set $\Omega$ in this time slab, retain the packets whose trajectories come within $s^{1+\eta}$ of $\Omega$ at the same time. For every $A>0$, the sum of the omitted packets is bounded pointwise on $\Omega$ by $C_{A,\eta}s^{-A}\\|v\\|_2$. For a partition into spatial squares of side $s^2$, extended over the common time slab, the retained data $v_Q$ can consequently be chosen so that $$\sum_Q\\|v_Q\\|_2^2\leq C\\|v\\|_2^2, \qquad \|Ev-Ev_Q\|\leq C_{A,\eta}s^{-A}\\|v\\|_2 \quad\text{on }Q\times[-Cs^2,Cs^2]. \label{eq:prelim-box-localization}$$ The same conclusions hold on translated time slabs after modulating the initial datum and writing the trajectories in the recentered time $t-t_0$.
  - Explanation: Exact packet decomposition with almost orthogonality, rapid tails and spatial localization of the retained data.
- **claim-refined-fractal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fractal)): Let $S\geq1$. Let $\mathcal Q$ be a collection of distinct unit lattice cubes in a ball of radius $CS$ in $\mathbb R^3$. Suppose that $$\#\{Q\in\mathcal Q:Q\cap B(z,r)\ne\varnothing\} \leq\gamma r^2\quad(1\leq r\leq CS), \qquad \#\{Q\in\mathcal Q:Q\cap B(z,\sqrt S)\ne\varnothing\} \leq\lambda_0 \label{eq:prelim-fractal-counts}$$ for all centers $z$, with fixed enlargements of the radius range allowed. If $v$ has frequency support in a fixed bounded set, then $$\begin{aligned} \int_{\bigcup\mathcal Q}\|Ev\|^2\,dx\,dt &\leq C_\epsilon S^\epsilon (\gamma\lambda_0S)^{1/3}\\|v\\|_2^2, \label{eq:prelim-fractal-integral}\\ \sum_{Q\in\mathcal Q}\sup_Q\|Ev\|^2 &\leq C_\epsilon S^\epsilon (\gamma\lambda_0S)^{1/3}\\|v\\|_2^2. \label{eq:prelim-fractal-sup} \end{aligned}$$ These estimates are uniform under translations of the collection of cubes.
  - Explanation: Refined fractal integral and unit-cube supremum estimates for cube collections with counting and occupancy bounds.
- **claim-weighted-refined** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-prelim-weighted)): Let $\nu$ be supported in a ball of radius $CS$, $S\geq1$, and suppose $$\nu(B(z,r))\leq mr^2\quad(r\geq1),\qquad \nu(B(z,\sqrt S))\leq mSd \quad(z\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nu), \label{eq:prelim-weighted-hypotheses}$$ where $m,d>0$. For bounded frequency support, $$\int \|Ev\|^2\,d\nu \leq C_\epsilon S^\epsilon mS^{2/3}d^{1/3}\\|v\\|_2^2. \label{eq:prelim-weighted-fractal}$$ The same bound holds for $\sum_Q\nu(Q)\sup_Q\|Ev\|^2$, summed over unit cubes. The ball bound alone gives this conclusion with $d=1$, up to a fixed constant.
  - Explanation: Weighted version of the refined estimate against a measure with a ball bound and a square-root-scale mass parameter.
- **claim-weighted-cap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cap)): Fix $\alpha_0<1/2$. Suppose that $S\geq1$, that $\nu$ is supported in a ball of radius $CS$ and satisfies $\nu(B(z,r))\leq mr^2$ for $r\geq1$, and that $v$ is supported in a frequency ball $B(v_0,q)$, with $v_0$ in a fixed bounded set and $$S^{-\alpha_0}\leq q\leq1.$$ Then, for every $\epsilon>0$, $$\int \|Ev\|^2\,d\nu \leq C_{\epsilon,\alpha_0}S^\epsilon mS^{2/3}q^{1/3}\\|v\\|_2^2. \label{eq:prelim-cap-estimate}$$ The estimate also holds with the integral replaced by $\sum_Q\nu(Q)\sup_Q\|Ev\|^2$ over unit cubes. Bounded translations, and averages of translations with a fixed integrable Schwartz weight, obey the same bounds.
  - Explanation: Weighted estimate for data supported in a frequency cap, gaining a power of the cap radius.
- **claim-transverse-power-saving** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-transverse)): Fix a bounded frequency region. There are $c>0$ and $L_0<\infty$, depending only on that region, for which the following configuration cannot exist when $L\ge L_0$. Let $\mathcal Q$ be unit lattice cubes inside a ball of radius $L^2$ in $\mathbb R^3$, with $$\#\mathcal Q\ge L^{4-c},\qquad \#\{Q\in\mathcal Q:Q\cap B(z,r)\ne\varnothing\} \le L^c r^2\quad(r\ge1),$$ where cube counts may equivalently use their centers, up to fixed constants. Suppose $v_1,v_2,v_3$ have frequency support in the fixed region, $\\|v_i\\|_2\le1$, and $$\sup_Q\|Ev_i\|\ge L^{-4/3-c} \qquad(Q\in\mathcal Q,\ i=1,2,3).$$ Then it is impossible that every triple of unit frequency directions, one from each support, has absolute determinant at least $L^{-c}$.
  - Explanation: A saturated configuration of cubes with three transverse extension inputs cannot exist for large scale.
- **claim-subpower-multilinear** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-product)): Suppose three exact extension inputs have bounded frequency supports, and every triple of their unit directions has determinant at least $\nu$, where $0<\nu\le1$. For each $\epsilon>0$ there are $C_\epsilon,M_\epsilon<\infty$ such that, on every ball of radius $S\ge2$, $$\label{eq:transverse-product} \int_{B_S}\prod_{i=1}^3\|Ev_i(z)\|\,dz \le C_\epsilon\nu^{-M_\epsilon}S^\epsilon \prod_{i=1}^3\\|v_i\\|_2.$$ The same conclusion holds for the sum over unit lattice cubes in the ball of $\prod_i\sup_Q\|Ev_i\|$. In particular, when $\nu=S^{-o(1)}$ both bounds are $S^{o(1)}\prod_i\\|v_i\\|_2$. For weighted tubes of width $\Delta$ in a bounded region, the corresponding multilinear Kakeya bound is $$\label{eq:transverse-kakeya} \int\prod_{i=1}^3 \left(\sum_T w_i(T)\mathbf1_T\right)^{1/2} \le C_\epsilon\nu^{-M_\epsilon}\Delta^{3-\epsilon} \prod_{i=1}^3\left(\sum_Tw_i(T)\right)^{1/2}.$$ Here fixed enlargements of tubes and of the ambient region are allowed; if those enlargements are subpower on a sequence, they cost a subpower.
  - Explanation: Multilinear restriction and Kakeya bounds with polynomial dependence on the transversality parameter.
- **claim-weighted-incident-tubes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-tubes)): A countersequence to Proposition <a href="#prop:transverse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transverse">8</a> yields a set $P$ in a fixed bounded region and three weighted tube families satisfying $$\label{eq:transverse-point-counts} \|P\|=L^{2+o(1)},\qquad \|P\cap B(z,r)\|\le L^{2+o(1)}r^2 \quad(L^{-1}\le r\le1).$$ The points are centers of distinct cubes of side comparable to $L^{-1}$. The tubes have width $L^{-1+o(1)}$, their coefficient weights obey $\sum_Tw_i(T)\lesssim1$, and every cross-family direction triple has determinant at least $L^{-o(1)}$. At each $p\in P$ there is a retained incident subfamily of total weight $e_i^*(p)=L^{-4/3+o(1)}$ such that its normalized weight law satisfies, for fixed $\kappa>0$, $u_0>0$, $$\label{eq:src1} \mathbb P\bigl(\operatorname{dir}T_i\in B(v,L^{-u})\mid X=p\bigr) \le L^{-\kappa u+o(1)} \qquad(0<u<u_0,\ u\in\mathbb Q).$$ This bound is uniform in $p,v$ for each fixed rational $u$. The retained subfamily may depend on $p$.
  - Explanation: A countersequence yields a point set and three weighted tube families with counting, weight and angular non-concentration properties.
- **claim-limiting-information** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-information)): After a subsequence, all the countably many information variables used below have a common limiting joint law. For spatial, coordinate, and direction partitions with at most $L^{C+o(1)}$ possible labels, their conditional informations are uniformly integrable and have bounded limiting values. In this law the chain rule remains exact and $$\label{eq:transverse-conditioning} I(Y\mid V)\ge I(Y\mid V,W)\quad\hbox{almost surely}.$$ If, given $Z,V$, the possible values of $Y$ on an event $E_L$ number at most $L^{\gamma+o(1)}$, include its indicator in the joint limit. On the event where the limiting indicator equals one, $I(Y\mid Z,V)\le\gamma$. Alternatively, an event specified by a finite system of sampled inequalities can be treated by open-set implications with fixed slack before that slack tends to zero; no identification of arbitrary boundary events is implicit. Consequently variables determining one another up to $L^{o(1)}$ possibilities have identical limiting information, also after additional conditioning. Finite systems of limiting inequalities holding with positive probability pass to finite $L$ with any fixed positive slack.
  - Explanation: A common limiting joint law for all information variables, with chain rule and conditioning inequalities preserved.
- **claim-spatial-profiles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-profiles)): In the limiting law set $$F(s)=I(X_s),\qquad G_i(s)=I(X_s\mid T_i),\qquad G_i'(s)=I(X_s\mid T_i').$$ These functions extend from rational scales to nondecreasing Lipschitz functions on $[0,1]$, vanishing at zero. Their Lipschitz constants are respectively $3$ and $1$. Almost surely, $$\label{eq:transverse-endpoint-information} F(s)\ge2s,\qquad F(1)=2,\qquad G_i(1),G_i'(1)\ge\tfrac23.$$
  - Explanation: Spatial information profiles are Lipschitz, vanish at zero and obey endpoint bounds.
- **claim-block-costs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-costs)): Write $J=F(s+t)-F(s)$ and $J_i=G_i(s+t)-G_i(s)$ for the chosen reference tubes. Define $$\ell=I(X_{s+t}\mid C)-I(X_{s+t}\mid C,D),\qquad \ell_i=I(X_{s+t}\mid C,T_i)-I(X_{s+t}\mid C,T_i,D).$$ All these limiting costs are nonnegative, and $$\label{eq:src2} \sum_{i=1}^3(J_i-\ell_i) \le\sum_{i=1}^3I(Z_i\mid Z_{\widehat i},C,D) \le I(Z\mid C,D)=J-\ell.$$ For disjoint blocks or disjoint prefixes, with the same $D$, the sum of expectations of each type of cost is $O(b_*)$. The same statement holds with any one fixed other tube in place of $T_i$. All coordinatewise deficits obtained by using any ordering in the second inequality of <a href="#eq:src2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src2">[eq:src2]</a> are nonnegative.
  - Explanation: Direction costs are nonnegative, compare block increments, and telescope with bounded expectation.
- **claim-additivity-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-additivity)): Almost surely, simultaneously for every choice of one of the two tubes in each family, $$F=G_1+G_2+G_3,\qquad G_i'=G_i,\qquad G_i(1)=\tfrac23.$$ In any block above, including a prefix and with any extra direction bins in $D$, $$\label{eq:src3} \begin{split} I(Z_i\mid C,D)&=J_i+O\left(\sum_k\ell_k\right),\\ I(Z_i,Z_j\mid C,D)&=J_i+J_j+ O\left(\sum_k\ell_k\right). \end{split}$$ The absolute constants in these error bounds are fixed.
  - Explanation: The total profile is the sum of the three tube profiles, and replacing a tube leaves its profile unchanged.
- **claim-common-profile** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-common-profile)): Almost surely, $$\label{eq:src4} G_1=G_2=G_3=:G,\qquad F=3G.$$ In particular $0\le\dot G\le1$, $G(1)=2/3$, and $3G(s)\ge2s$.
  - Explanation: All three tube profiles coincide and the total profile is three times the common one.
- **claim-robust-projection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-projection)): Fix $0<d<1$ and $\rho_0>0$. There are $\varepsilon_{\rm pr}>0$ and $\eta_{\rm pr}>0$ with the following property for all sufficiently small $\Delta$. Suppose $A\subset B(0,1)\subset\mathbb R^2$, with $\mathcal N_\Delta$ denoting covering number by radius-$\Delta$ balls, satisfies $$\Delta^{-2d+\eta_{\rm pr}}\le\mathcal N_\Delta(A) \le\Delta^{-2d-\eta_{\rm pr}},\qquad \mathcal N_\Delta(A\cap B(z,r)) \le\Delta^{-\eta_{\rm pr}}r^{\rho_0} \mathcal N_\Delta(A)$$ for $\Delta\le r\le1$. Let $\sigma$ be a probability on projection lines obeying $\sigma(B(V,r))\le\Delta^{-\eta_{\rm pr}}r^{\rho_0}$ on the same range. Some $V\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma$ then satisfies $$\mathcal N_\Delta(\pi_V A')\ge\Delta^{-d-\varepsilon_{\rm pr}} \quad\hbox{for every }A'\subset A\hbox{ with } \mathcal N_\Delta(A')\ge\Delta^{\eta_{\rm pr}} \mathcal N_\Delta(A).$$ One may decrease both the tolerance and the gain.
  - Explanation: Robust planar discretized projection: some direction in the support improves the projection of every large subset.
- **claim-angular-marginal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-angular-information)): The functions $h_2,h_3$ are nonnegative and nondecreasing on their rational domains, and almost surely $$\label{eq:transverse-angular-sum} h_2(u)+h_3(u)\ge\kappa u\qquad(0<u<u_0,\ u\in\mathbb Q).$$ Their values agree with those of marginal coordinate-cell information at comparable resolution; the analogous joint assertion also holds.
  - Explanation: Monotone angular marginal informations whose sum is bounded below linearly.
- **claim-projection-block-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-block-test)): Fix $0<d<1$, $d_0>0$, and $\kappa_0>0$. There is $\zeta>0$, depending only on these parameters, for which the following conditions cannot hold on an event of positive limiting probability. Fix rational $[s,s+b]\subset[0,1]$, with $b<u_0$, rational $b_*\ge b$, and $j\in\{2,3\}$. At direction precision $L^{-b_*}$ let $D_0$ be the reference triple of bins and let $D_1$ add the alternative bin. Require* 1. *$\|G(s+b)-G(s)-db\|\le\zeta b$, and $G(s+u)-G(s)\ge d_0u-\zeta b$;* 2. *all costs in Lemma <a href="#lem:transverse-costs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transverse-costs">13</a>, for both $D_0$ and $D_1$ and each reference tube or $T_1'$, are at most $\zeta b$;* 3. *$h_j(u)\ge\kappa_0u-\zeta b$.* *The prefix, cost, and angular requirements need be imposed only on a fixed finite rational grid of $0<u\le b$ containing $b$, with mesh at most a sufficiently small fixed multiple of $\zeta b$. Decreasing $\zeta$ preserves the conclusion. All power-error constants are independent of $s,b,b_*$; the meshes can be fixed in relative coordinates $u/b$, and their finite cardinalities only affect fixed constants in the later cost sums.
  - Explanation: Block conditions combining spatial, cost and angular hypotheses cannot hold on a positive-probability event.
- **claim-finite-angular-alternatives** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-angular-choice)): Fix $0<d<1$ and $d_0>0$. There is a fixed finite list of positive rational ratios, with minimum $c_*>0$, such that for every sufficiently small rational $b_0$, almost every path has a choice $b'$ from this list times $b_0$ and a marginal $j\in\{2,3\}$ satisfying the angular hypothesis of Lemma <a href="#lem:transverse-block-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transverse-block-test">18</a>, with spare slack, for block lengths in $[b'/4,b']$. Each alternative has its own fixed positive $\kappa_0$, tolerance, and finite relative mesh. All choices are independent of $b_0$, and remain valid under sufficiently small variations of the spatial increment parameters.
  - Explanation: A finite list of scale ratios guarantees one of two angular marginals meets the block-test hypothesis.
- **claim-binary-derivative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-binary)): For almost every path, $\dot G(s)\in\{0,1\}$ for almost every $s\in[0,1]$.
  - Explanation: The common profile has derivative equal to zero or one almost everywhere.
- **claim-balanced-hills** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-hills)): Let $G$ be Lipschitz with derivative $\mathbf1_E$, and let $a<b$ be respectively a density point of $E$ and of its complement. There is a positive number $b_{\mathrm{hill}}$ such that, for every $0<r<b_{\mathrm{hill}}$, some $[x,y]\subset(a,b)$ has $$r\le y-x\le2r,\qquad G(y)-G(x)=\tfrac12(y-x),\qquad G(x+u)-G(x)\ge\tfrac12u\quad(0\le u\le y-x).$$ For any fixed finite collection of disjoint ordered density pairs, these intervals can be chosen disjointly at every sufficiently small common $r$.
  - Explanation: Balanced subintervals of prescribed slope exist between density points of a set and of its complement.
- **claim-finite-jumps** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-finite-jumps)): Almost surely $E$ agrees modulo null sets with a finite union of intervals and has an interior transition from an interval of full measure to an interval of zero measure. For every fixed $\eta>0$ there is a rational block $[s,s+b]$, $0<b<u_0$, on an event of positive probability, such that $$\label{eq:transverse-jump-block} G(s+b)-G(s)=\tfrac b2+O(\eta b),\qquad G(s+b/2)-G(s)=\tfrac b2+O(\eta b).$$ The absolute error constants can be fixed independently of $\eta$.
  - Explanation: Finitely many alternations and an interior full-to-empty transition, giving a block with the jump increments.
- **claim-no-full-to-empty-block** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-no-jump)): For sufficiently small fixed $\eta>0$, the positive-probability configuration in <a href="#eq:transverse-jump-block" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transverse-jump-block">[eq:transverse-jump-block]</a> is impossible.
  - Explanation: The full-to-empty block configuration is impossible, by polynomial partitioning and the angular law.
- **claim-sparse-packet-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-sparse)): There is an absolute constant $c_s>0$ with the following property. Fix $\tau_0>0$, and let $L^{\tau_0}\le D\le L$. Let $U$ be a finite packet array of the form <a href="#eq:src5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src5">[eq:src5]</a>, with coefficient vector $c$, and let $\mu$ be a nonnegative measure such that $$\mu(B(z,r))\le m r^2\quad(r\ge1),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu\subset\{\|t\|\le C L^2\}.$$ Assume the packet sparsity condition <a href="#eq:src6" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src6">[eq:src6]</a>, with $M=100$, for every label with nonzero coefficient. Then $$\label{eq:src7} \sum_{q\text{ a unit lattice cube}} \mu(q)^{2/3}\sup_q\|U\|^2 \lesssim_{\tau_0} m^{2/3}L^{4/3}D^{-c_s}\\|c\\|_{\ell^2}^2.$$ Only finitely many uniform profile derivative bounds, depending on $\tau_0$, are required. Consequently the same assertion holds for any prescribed finite set of field derivatives, provided the original profiles have sufficiently many bounded derivatives. Fixed enlargements of the time range and restriction to a submeasure are allowed. The exponent $c_s$ is independent of $\tau_0$; the implicit constant and the required differentiability need not be.
  - Explanation: Sparse packet arrays gain a negative power of the sparsity parameter over the natural scaling bound.
- **claim-exact-fourier-layers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-layers)): Fix a bounded native time interval $\|\sigma\|\le C_{\rm nat}$, any finite number of profile seminorms on that interval, and any prescribed finite spatial decay orders there. After requiring sufficiently many derivatives of the compact profiles, an array of atoms in <a href="#eq:src5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src5">[eq:src5]</a> is a sum-integral of exact extension arrays, indexed by $k\in\mathbb Z^2$ and $u\in\mathbb R$, times a common phase $e^{iu t/L^2}$. There are nonnegative common weights $w(k,u)$, with $$\sum_k\int_\mathbb Rw(k,u)\,du<\infty,$$ such that division by $w(k,u)$ gives all the stipulated uniform seminorm and decay bounds. For every subarray $A$ the frequency input $v_{A,k,u}$ of its layer satisfies $$\label{eq:sparse-layer-energy} \\|v_{A,k,u}\\|_2^2 \le C w(k,u)^2\sum_{\alpha\in A}\|c_\alpha\|^2.$$ Its exact frequencies lie within $O(L^{-1})$ of $\omega_\alpha+k/L$ for the corresponding labels.
  - Explanation: Compact atom arrays are superpositions of exact extension layers with rapidly decaying common weights.
- **claim-broad-saving** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-broad)): There is an absolute $c_b>0$ such that, whenever $K\le s^{c_b}$, the broad part of the functional in a ball of radius $O(s^2)$ satisfies $$\label{eq:sparse-broad} \sum_{Q\text{ broad}}\nu(Q)^{2/3}\\|V\\|_{L^6(Q)}^2 \lesssim s^{-c_b}m_0^{2/3}s^{4/3}\mathcal E.$$ Here $\nu(B(z,r))\le m_0r^2$ for $r\ge1$, and the exact frequency data for $V$ and each $V_\theta$ have $L^2$ norms at most $C\mathcal E^{1/2}$. The implicit constant can depend on this fixed $C$ but $c_b$ does not depend on packet differentiability.
  - Explanation: The broad part of the functional gains an absolute power when the cap parameter is small relative to the scale.
- **claim-bilinear-frame** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-frame)): Fix bounded frequency and profile boxes, a bound on phase-space multiplicity, and a sufficiently large envelope exponent $P$. There is a fixed finite number of profile derivatives for which the following holds uniformly. Let $h\geq1$ and $h^{-1/2}\leq\delta\lesssim1$. For $i=1,2$, let $$V_i(x,t)=\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal I_i}s_\ell e^{i(x\xi_\ell-t\xi_\ell^2)} H_\ell\left(\frac{x-b_\ell-2\xi_\ell t}{h},\frac{t}{h^2}\right)$$ be a finite packet sum. The profiles are supported in the fixed box and have the stipulated uniform derivative bounds. The centers $b_\ell$ may be arbitrary real numbers, and the pairs $(b_\ell/h,h\xi_\ell)$ have bounded multiplicity in every unit phase-space ball. The frequencies of the two fields lie in intervals of length $\delta$ with distance at least a fixed positive multiple of $\delta$. Put $$S_i(x,t)^2=\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal I_i}\|s_\ell\|^2 \left(1+\frac{\|x-b_\ell-2\xi_\ell t\|}{h} +\frac{\|t\|}{h^2}\right)^{-P}, \qquad H_i^*=h^3\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal I_i}\|s_\ell\|^2.$$ For $A_i,B_i>0$, write $\alpha_i=A_i/B_i$. If $\alpha_i\geq C$, for a sufficiently large fixed $C$, the number of unit lattice squares containing a point where simultaneously $\|V_i\|\geq A_i$ and $S_i\leq B_i$ for $i=1,2$ satisfies $$\label{eq:src8} \#\leq C'\delta^{-C'}\alpha_1^{-2}\alpha_2^{-2} \left(\frac{H_1^*}{A_1^2}+\frac{H_2^*}{A_2^2}\right).$$ The constants depend only on the fixed bounds just specified. In particular, they are independent of $h$, the number of packets, and the number of scales in the proof. The loss in the separation parameter has at most a fixed polynomial power.
  - Explanation: Bound on the number of unit squares with simultaneously large fields and small envelopes, uniform in the number of scales.
- **claim-frame-initial** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frame-initial)): It suffices to prove <a href="#eq:src8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src8">[eq:src8]</a> for packets whose spatial frequencies are supported in caps of width $O(h^{-1})$ about their assigned centers, whose spatial profiles have sufficiently rapid polynomial decay, and whose native time supports remain bounded. After dividing a field by its envelope bound $B$, its witness level is $\alpha=A/B$ and its envelope is at most one. Given $$\lambda=C_*/\alpha\leq1,$$ deleting all packets of normalized amplitude greater than $\lambda$ changes the field at every witness by at most $C\lambda^{-1}$. The remaining field $g_0$ has an energy parameter $D\lesssim H^*/B^2$ such that $\\|g_0\\|_2^2\leq D$. At a dyadic scale $\Delta_0\asymp h^{-1}$, its fine cap pieces $g_{0,b}$ satisfy, for all fixed orders needed below, $$\begin{aligned} \\| (\partial_x-ib)^sD_b^r g_{0,b}\\|_\infty &\leq C_{r,s}\lambda\Delta_0^{s+2r}, \label{eq:frame-initial-sup}\\ \sum_b\\|(\partial_x-ib)^sD_b^r g_{0,b}\\|_2^2 &\leq C_{r,s}\Delta_0^{2s+4r}D. \label{eq:frame-initial-square} \end{aligned}$$ In particular $\\|\mathcal L^r g_0\\|_2\lesssim_r \Delta_0^{2r}\sqrt D$.
  - Explanation: Reduction to cap-supported layers with normalized envelope and initial deletion of large amplitudes.
- **claim-frame-masks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frame-masks)): The masks can be chosen so that $$\label{eq:src15} \|b_a\|\leq\lambda,\qquad \\|\mathcal L P_ab_a\\|_\infty\leq C_1\lambda\Delta^2,$$ where $C_1$ is independent of the large fixed $K$. For every required fixed derivative order, $$\label{eq:src16} \\|D_a^rP_ab_a\\|_\infty\leq C_{r,K}\lambda\Delta^{2r}, \qquad \int_a\\|D_a^rP_ab_a\\|_2^2\leq C_{r,K}\Delta^{4r}D.$$ The removed pieces obey $$\label{eq:src17} \begin{split} \\|(\partial_x-ia)^sD_a^rd_a\\|_1 &\leq C_{K,r,s}\Delta^{s+2r}\lambda^{-1}\int L_a,\\ \\|(\partial_x-ia)^sD_a^rd_a\\|_2^2 &\leq C_{K,r,s}\Delta^{2s+4r}\int L_a, \end{split}$$ and, pointwise, $$\label{eq:src18} \|R_j\|\leq C_K\lambda^{-1}W_jL_j.$$ All derivative constants are uniform in the stage number.
  - Explanation: Smooth masks keep amplitudes bounded with controlled curvature and loss bounds, uniformly in stage.
- **claim-frame-flux** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frame-flux)): For a smooth spatial projection $\operatorname{Proj}_r$ to $\|\xi\|\asymp r$, where $r\lesssim\Delta_j$, $$\label{eq:src20} \\|\operatorname{Proj}_rW_j\Phi_j\\|_2^2 \leq C_K\lambda^2(r/\Delta_j)l_j.$$
  - Explanation: Low-frequency flux of the deletion errors carries a gain proportional to the frequency ratio.
- **claim-frame-quartic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frame-quartic)): Let $G_j(u)=\exp(-cu^2/\Delta_j^2)$, for a fixed $c>0$. Then $$\label{eq:src22} \sum_{j=1}^n\int_{\mathbb R^2}(G_j-G_{j-1})(u) \|\widehat{\|g_{j-1}\|^2}(u,\tau)\|^2\,du\,d\tau \lesssim_K\lambda^2D.$$
  - Explanation: A positive quartic potential controls the changes of smoothing scale across all stages.
- **claim-frame-maximal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frame-maximal)): The partial errors obey <a href="#eq:src19" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src19">[eq:src19]</a>. If $\mathcal M$ is the spacetime Hardy–Littlewood maximal operator, then also $$\label{eq:src23} \|Y_k(z')\|\lesssim (1+\Delta_k\|z-z'\|)^C\mathcal M(\sup_j\|Y_j\|)(z)+C_K\lambda^2.$$
  - Explanation: Partial error sums are recovered without a logarithm and obey a pointwise maximal comparison.
- **claim-frame-local-bilinear** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frame-local-bilinear)): Consider the two states from the preceding construction with $0<\lambda_i\leq1$. Let $Q$ be a square of side $\Delta_k^{-1}$ and let $\mathcal Z_Q\subset Q$ contain at most one designated point from each unit lattice square. Suppose both states $g_{k-1}^{(i)}$ have modulus at least $c\alpha_i$ at these points. There is a Schwartz weight $\eta_Q$, bounded below on $Q$ and with Fourier support of radius $O(\Delta_k)$, for which $$\label{eq:frame-local-sampling} \alpha_1^2\alpha_2^2\#\mathcal Z_Q \lesssim_K\delta^{-C}\int_{\mathbb R^2}\|\eta_Q(z')\|^2 \prod_{i=1}^2\left(W_{k-1}\|g_{k-1}^{(i)}\|^2(z')+1\right)\,dz',$$ provided the ratio constants are sufficiently large. The two frequency intervals must retain a gap comparable to $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Local bilinear sampling bound for designated points of two separated states.
- **claim-oned-flat-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-oned)): Let $\nu$ be a nonnegative measure supported in a spatial interval of length $O(L^2)$ and in $\|t\|\leq L^2$. Suppose that, for every $t_0,b\in\mathbb R$, every $v\in I$, and every $1\leq\rho\leq r\leq L^2$, $$\label{eq:flat-tube-test} \nu\bigl\{(x,t): \|t-t_0\|\leq r, \ \|x-b-2vt\|\leq\rho\bigr\} \leq m_1 r\left(1+\frac{\rho}{L}\right).$$ There is an exponent $p_0>2$, for which one may take $p_0=21/10$, such that every array <a href="#eq:flat-oned-field" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flat-oned-field">[eq:flat-oned-field]</a> and every $g>0$ satisfy $$\label{eq:src9} \nu\{\|V\|\geq gL^{-5/6}\} \leq C m_1 L^3 g^{-p_0}\\|c\\|_2^{p_0}.$$ The constant is uniform in $L$, in the number of packets, and in their real centers. In particular, no factor growing with $L$ is allowed.
  - Explanation: Distribution estimate for one-dimensional packet arrays against measures with a moving-tube bound, uniform in all array data.
- **claim-thin-strip-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-strip)): Let $U$ be a finite two-dimensional packet array of the form <a href="#eq:src5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src5">[eq:src5]</a>, with the fixed profile and phase-space bounds stated there. Suppose all frequency labels lie within distance $C/L$ of an affine line in frequency space. Let $\mu$ be supported in a spatial square of side $O(L^2)$ and in $\|t\|\leq L^2$, and suppose $\mu(B_r)\leq mr^2$ for every ball with arbitrary center and every $r\geq1$. Then, with the exponent $p_0$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:oned" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:oned">36</a>, $$\label{eq:src10} \mu\{\|U\|\geq gL^{-4/3}\} \leq C mL^4g^{-p_0}\\|c\\|_2^{p_0},\qquad g>0.$$ The constant depends only on the fixed bounds, including the strip width constant. No sparsity hypothesis is required.
  - Explanation: Two-dimensional arrays with frequencies near an affine line obey the same exponent without a sparsity hypothesis.
- **claim-rich-tube-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-rich)): Fix bounded velocity, domain, ball, and neighborhood constants, a richness constant $c_{\mathrm r}>0$, an angular constant $C_{\mathrm a}>0$, exponents $A_{\mathrm r},A_{\mathrm a}\geq0$, and $\eta>0$. There are constants $A_*,C_*,C'$ depending only on these fixed data such that the following holds whenever $X\geq2$ and $H\geq C_*X^{A_*}$.* *Let $\nu$ be a nonnegative measure supported in a ball of radius $O(H)$, with $$\nu(B(q,r))\leq C_{\mathrm b}r^2\qquad(r\geq1).$$ Let $\mathcal T$ be a finite family of lines $$\ell_T=\{(b_T+2\omega_Tt,t):t\in\mathbb R\},\qquad \|\omega_T\|\leq C_{\mathrm v},$$ viewed as unit-width tubes over a fixed time interval of length $O(H)$ containing the time projection of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nu$. Write $d_T(x,t)=\|x-b_T-2\omega_Tt\|$ and $e_T=(2\omega_T,1)/\|(2\omega_T,1)\|$. Assume:* 1. *Every tube is rich against $\nu$: $$\label{eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis} \int (1+d_T(q))^{-M}\,d\nu(q) \geq c_{\mathrm r} H X^{-A_{\mathrm r}},\qquad M=100.$$* 2. *At $\nu$-almost every $q$ there is a measurably chosen probability $(p_T(q))_{T\in\mathcal T}$, supported on tubes with $d_T(q)\leq C_{\mathrm n}$, such that every spherical direction ball satisfies $$\label{eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis} \sum_{e_T\in B_{\mathbb S^2}(e,r)}p_T(q) \leq C_{\mathrm a}X^{A_{\mathrm a}}r^\eta \qquad(r\geq H^{-1}).$$* *Then each tube can be assigned to one of at most $X^{C'}$ plates (planes thickened by $X^{C'}$). Its assigned plate contains its central line segment over the relevant time interval. If $n$ is a unit normal to that plate, then $$\label{eq:src11} \|n\cdot(2\omega_T,1)\|\leq X^{C'}/H.$$ The constants are independent of the number of tubes. In particular, the richness in <a href="#eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis">[eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis]</a> and the pointwise law in <a href="#eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis">[eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis]</a> use exactly the same measure $\nu$.
  - Explanation: Tubes that are rich against a measure and have spread directions are assigned to few plates containing their lines.
- **claim-truncated-parameter-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-geometry-energy)): Let $H\geq1$ and let $\sigma$ be a finite measure on $\mathbb R^2$ of mass $m$, satisfying $$\sigma(B(z,r))\leq A r^{1+\eta} \qquad(z\in\mathbb R^2,\ r\geq H^{-1}),\qquad A>0.$$ Set $\gamma=\eta/(1+\eta)$. Then $$\label{eq:geometry-parameter-energy} \iint \min\{H,\|z-z'\|^{-1}\}\,d\sigma(z')\,d\sigma(z) \leq C_\eta A^{1/(1+\eta)}m^{1+\gamma},$$ where the kernel on the diagonal is $H$.
  - Explanation: Truncated energy bound for a finite measure satisfying a Frostman-type condition above a cutoff scale.
- **claim-dyadic-distribution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-distribution)): There are fixed $p>2$ and $\sigma>0$ with the following property. Let $U$ be a finite sum of the atoms in <a href="#eq:src5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src5">[eq:src5]</a>, of packet size $L\geq1$, with $\sum_\alpha\|c_\alpha\|^2\leq1$. Suppose that $\mu$ is supported in a spatial box of side $O(L^2)$ and in $\|t\|\leq L^2$, that $\mu(B_r)\leq mr^2$ for every ball of radius $r\geq1$, and that <a href="#eq:src6" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src6">[eq:src6]</a> holds for every label, with $1\leq D\leq L$. Then, for every $g>0$, $$\label{eq:src12} \mu(A)\leq C mL^4D^{-\sigma}g^{-p}, \qquad A=\{z:\|U(z)\|\geq\lambda\},\qquad \lambda=gL^{-4/3}.$$ The constant is uniform in $L,D,g,m$ and the size of the array. It may depend on the fixed packet class, including sufficiently many finite profile derivative bounds. For an arbitrary coefficient array, the right side is multiplied by $\\|c\\|_{\ell^2}^p$.
  - Explanation: Dyadic distribution bound for level sets of packet sums with a positive saving in the sparsity parameter and exponent above two.
- **claim-dyadic-cap-gain** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dyadic-cap-gain)): For any subarray in a frequency square of side comparable to $r$, where $L^{-1}\lesssim r\leq\delta_0$, outer induction gives $$\label{eq:dyadic-cap-gain} \mu\{\|U_{\mathcal C}\|\geq r^\eta\lambda\} \leq C_{\rm cap}C_{\rm ind}mL^4g^{-p} r^{1-p/3-p\eta} \Big(\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal C}\|c_\alpha\|^2\Big)^{p/2}.$$ The estimate holds for any submeasure of $\mu$, with the same upper ball parameter. At the smallest scales choose comparable squares so that the child packet size is at least $1$.
  - Explanation: Rescaling to a frequency square gives a power gain through outer induction.
- **claim-energy-truncation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dyadic-prefix)): Let a finite coefficient array be ordered, with total energy $E$, and let each output point select a prefix ending between whole coefficients. Suppose the distribution estimate for every block, on the submeasure where that block is selected, has right side $K t^{-p}\\|c_{\rm block}\\|_{\ell^2}^p$, where $p>2$. The field formed from the selected prefix then satisfies the same estimate with right side $C_p Kt^{-p}E^{p/2}$.
  - Explanation: Block estimates transfer to prefix fields with the total energy and no loss in the number of coefficients.
- **claim-angular-test-flow** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dyadic-flow)): Outside the exceptions in <a href="#eq:dyadic-prefix-exception" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dyadic-prefix-exception">[eq:dyadic-prefix-exception]</a> and <a href="#eq:dyadic-cap-exceptions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dyadic-cap-exceptions">[eq:dyadic-cap-exceptions]</a>, an original point cannot exit in an angular deletion. On a surviving such point, $\|U_{\mathcal R}\|\geq c\lambda$ for an absolute $c>0$.
  - Explanation: Outside the exceptional sets a point cannot be removed by the angular test, by a cut-capacity argument on the direction tree.
- **claim-flattened-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dyadic-flattening)): For a time interval $J$ of length $h^2$, let $\mathcal T(J)$ denote the boundedly many native time-cell layers that can contribute on $J$. Summing over the disjoint fine arrays, and also over clusters when needed, one has $$\label{eq:src13} \begin{aligned} \sum_{\theta,\,T\in\mathcal T(J)}s_{\theta,T}^2 &\lesssim h^{-2},\\ \sum_{\theta,T}d(T)^{2/3}s_{\theta,T}^2 &\lesssim_B h^{-2}a^{4/3}D^{-c_s}. \end{aligned}$$ The right sides are multiplied by the total original coefficient energy if it is not normalized to be at most $1$.
  - Explanation: Energy bounds for composite packets, locally in time and weighted by density over the full time range.
- **claim-tuple-frame-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dyadic-tuple)): For every tuple remaining after <a href="#eq:dyadic-small-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dyadic-small-mass">[eq:dyadic-small-mass]</a>, $$\label{eq:dyadic-frame-substitution} M_0\lesssim \delta_0^{-C}mh \frac{B_1^2B_2^2}{A_{\ell_1}^2A_{\ell_2}^2} \left(\frac{h^3E_1}{A_{\ell_1}^2} +\frac{h^3E_2}{A_{\ell_2}^2}\right).$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:dyadic-cubic-mass} \lambda^6M_0^3\lesssim \delta_0^{-C}(1+\ell_1+\ell_2)^C m^3h^{10}d_1d_2E_1E_2(E_1+E_2).$$
  - Explanation: For each remaining tuple the frame estimate bounds the witness mass and yields a cubic bound in the class energies.
- **claim-short-time-maximal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-shortmax)): Let $p>2$ be the exponent in Theorem <a href="#thm:distribution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:distribution">41</a>. Fix $0<c_0<C_0<\infty$. If $N\ge1$ is dyadic and $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\widehat f_N\subset \{\xi\in\mathbb R^2:c_0N\le \|\xi\|\le C_0N\},$$ then, on every unit ball $B\subset\mathbb R^2$, $$\left\\|\sup_{0<t<1/N}\|S(t)f_N\|\right\\|_{L^{p,\infty}(B)} \le C N^{1/3}\\|f_N\\|_2. \label{eq:src24}$$ The same estimate holds on every translated time interval of length at most $1/N$, with either endpoint included.
  - Explanation: Weak-type short-time maximal estimate for annular frequency data with the endpoint power of the frequency.
- **claim-time-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoint-time-separation)): Let $\mathcal D_j$ be the dyadic partition of $[0,1)$ into intervals of length $2^{-j}=1/N$. Then $$e_j([0,1))\le C\sum_{I\in\mathcal D_j}e_j(I)+CN^{-2}. \label{eq:endpoint-time-almost-orthogonality}$$
  - Explanation: Almost orthogonality of the dual functions across dyadic time intervals up to a small error.
- **claim-mass-spatial-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoint-localized-dual)): If $I\in\mathcal D_j$ and $J\subset I$ is a dyadic descendant at relative depth $k$, where $0\le k\le j$, set $r=2^{-k}$. Then $$\begin{aligned} e_j(J)&\le C m_J^{2-2/p}, \label{eq:endpoint-mass-bound}\\ e_j(J)&\le C r^{2/3}m_J. \label{eq:endpoint-spatial-bound} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently, with $$\alpha=\frac32-\frac1p=1+\frac{1-2/p}{2}>1,$$ we have $$e_j(J)\le C2^{-k/3}m_J^\alpha. \label{eq:endpoint-interpolated-dual}$$
  - Explanation: Mass and spatial localization bounds interpolate to a superlinear power of the mass with decay in relative depth.
- **claim-common-tree-summation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoint-square-sum)): For every graph and phase choice in <a href="#eq:endpoint-dual-definitions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:endpoint-dual-definitions">[eq:endpoint-dual-definitions]</a>, $$\sum_{j\ge1}2^{-2j/3}\\|G_{2^j}([0,1))\\|_2^2\le C. \label{eq:endpoint-square-sum}$$ The constant is independent of $t$, $w$, and the center of $\Omega$.
  - Explanation: The weighted sum of dual energies over all frequency scales is bounded uniformly, using a common time tree and a convexity deficit.
- **claim-local-strong-maximal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-maximal)): There is a constant $C$ such that, for every $f\in H^{1/3}(\mathbb R^2)$ whose Fourier transform has compact support and every $x_0\in\mathbb R^2$, $$\int_{B(x_0,1)}\sup_{0\le t<1}\|S(t)f(x)\|\,dx \le C\\|f\\|_{H^{1/3}}. \label{eq:endpoint-strong-maximal}$$ For arbitrary $f\in H^{1/3}(\mathbb R^2)$ there is a jointly measurable representative $v_f(x,t)$, $0\le t<1$, continuous in $t$ for almost every $x$, equal to $S(t)f$ as an $L^2$ function for each fixed $t$, and satisfying the same bound with $S(t)f(x)$ replaced by $v_f(x,t)$.
  - Explanation: Local integral bound for the maximal function by the Sobolev norm, and a representative continuous in time almost everywhere.
- **claim-gaussian-lebesgue-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoint-heat-envelope)): Let $h\ge0$ be locally integrable on $\mathbb R^2$ and suppose $$A=\sup_{z\in\mathbb R^2}\int_{B(z,1)}h(y)\,dy<\infty.$$ For $a>0$ let $$K_a(z)=\frac1{4\pi a}e^{-\|z\|^2/(4a)}.$$ Then $(K_a*h)(x)$ is finite for every $a>0$ and every $x$. At every Lebesgue point $x$ of $h$, $$\lim_{a\downarrow0}(K_a*h)(x)=h(x). \label{eq:endpoint-heat-envelope-limit}$$
  - Explanation: Gaussian averages of a function with uniformly bounded local mass converge at every Lebesgue point.

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fractal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S\\geq1$. Let $\\mathcal Q$ be a collection of distinct unit lattice cubes in a ball of radius $CS$ in $\\mathbb R^3$. Suppose that $$\\#\\{Q\\in\\mathcal Q:Q\\cap B(z,r)\\ne\\varnothing\\}\n \\leq\\gamma r^2\\quad(1\\leq r\\leq CS),\n \\qquad\n \\#\\{Q\\in\\mathcal Q:Q\\cap B(z,\\sqrt S)\\ne\\varnothing\\}\n \\leq\\lambda_0\n \\label{eq:prelim-fractal-counts}$$ for all centers $z$, with fixed enlargements of the radius range allowed. If $v$ has frequency support in a fixed bounded set, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{\\bigcup\\mathcal Q}|Ev|^2\\,dx\\,dt\n &\\leq C_\\epsilon S^\\epsilon\n       (\\gamma\\lambda_0S)^{1/3}\\|v\\|_2^2,\n \\label{eq:prelim-fractal-integral}\\\\\n \\sum_{Q\\in\\mathcal Q}\\sup_Q|Ev|^2\n &\\leq C_\\epsilon S^\\epsilon\n       (\\gamma\\lambda_0S)^{1/3}\\|v\\|_2^2.\n \\label{eq:prelim-fractal-sup}\n\\end{aligned}$$ These estimates are uniform under translations of the collection of cubes.",
          "source_id": "PM-080.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $S\\geq1$. Let $\\mathcal Q$ be a collection of distinct unit lattice cubes in a ball of radius $CS$ in $\\mathbb R^3$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-prelim-weighted",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu$ be supported in a ball of radius $CS$, $S\\geq1$, and suppose $$\\nu(B(z,r))\\leq mr^2\\quad(r\\geq1),\\qquad\n \\nu(B(z,\\sqrt S))\\leq mSd\n \\quad(z\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\nu),\n \\label{eq:prelim-weighted-hypotheses}$$ where $m,d>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cap",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\alpha_0<1/2$. Suppose that $S\\geq1$, that $\\nu$ is supported in a ball of radius $CS$ and satisfies $\\nu(B(z,r))\\leq mr^2$ for $r\\geq1$, and that $v$ is supported in a frequency ball $B(v_0,q)$, with $v_0$ in a fixed bounded set and $$S^{-\\alpha_0}\\leq q\\leq1.$$ Then, for every $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\int |Ev|^2\\,d\\nu\n \\leq C_{\\epsilon,\\alpha_0}S^\\epsilon\n       mS^{2/3}q^{1/3}\\|v\\|_2^2.\n \\label{eq:prelim-cap-estimate}$$ The estimate also holds with the integral replaced by $\\sum_Q\\nu(Q)\\sup_Q|Ev|^2$ over unit cubes.         "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-transverse",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a bounded frequency region. There are $c>0$ and $L_0<\\infty$, depending only on that region, for which the following configuration cannot exist when $L\\ge L_0$. Let $\\mathcal Q$ be unit lattice cubes inside a ball of radius $L^2$ in $\\mathbb R^3$, with $$\\#\\mathcal Q\\ge L^{4-c},\\qquad\n \\#\\{Q\\in\\mathcal Q:Q\\cap B(z,r)\\ne\\varnothing\\}\n       \\le L^c r^2\\quad(r\\ge1),$$ where cube counts may equivalently use their centers, up to fixed constants. Suppose $v_1,v_2,v_3$ have frequency support in the fixed region, $\\|v_i\\|_2\\le1$, and $$\\sup_Q|Ev_i|\\ge L^{-4/3-c}\n \\qquad(Q\\in\\mathcal Q,\\ i=1,2,3).$$ Then it is impossible that every triple of unit frequency directions, one from each support, has absolute determinant at least $L^{-c}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Suppose $v_1,v_2,v_3$ have frequency support in the fixed region, $\\|v_i\\|_2\\le1$, and $$\\sup_Q|Ev_i|\\ge L^{-4/3-c}\n \\qquad(Q\\in\\mathcal Q,\\ i=1,2,3).$$ Then it is impossible that every triple of unit frequency directions, one from each support, has absolute determinant at least $L^{-c}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-080.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-product",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose three exact extension inputs have bounded frequency supports, and every triple of their unit directions has determinant at least $\\nu$, where $0<\\nu\\le1$. For each $\\epsilon>0$ there are $C_\\epsilon,M_\\epsilon<\\infty$ such that, on every ball of radius $S\\ge2$, $$\\label{eq:transverse-product}\n \\int_{B_S}\\prod_{i=1}^3|Ev_i(z)|\\,dz\n \\le C_\\epsilon\\nu^{-M_\\epsilon}S^\\epsilon\n                         \\prod_{i=1}^3\\|v_i\\|_2.$$ The same conclusion holds for the sum over unit lattice cubes in the ball of $\\prod_i\\sup_Q|Ev_i|$. In particular, when $\\nu=S^{-o(1)}$ both bounds are $S^{o(1)}\\prod_i\\|v_i\\|_2$. For weighted tubes of width $\\Delta$ in a bounded region, the corresponding multilinear Kakeya bound is $$\\label{eq:transverse-kakeya}\n \\int\\prod_{i=1}^3\n       \\left(\\sum_T w_i(T)\\mathbf1_T\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le C_\\epsilon\\nu^{-M_\\epsilon}\\Delta^{3-\\epsilon}\n                  \\prod_{i=1}^3\\left(\\sum_Tw_i(T)\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Here fixed enlargements of tubes and of the ambient region are allowed; if those enlargements are subpower on a sequence, they cost a subpower.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-product",

In particular, when $\\nu=S^{-o(1)}$ both bounds are $S^{o(1)}\\prod_i\\|v_i\\|_2$. For weighted tubes of width $\\Delta$ in a bounded region, the corresponding multilinear Kakeya bound is $$\\label{eq:transverse-kakeya}\n \\int\\prod_{i=1}^3\n       \\left(\\sum_T w_i(T)\\mathbf1_T\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le C_\\epsilon\\nu^{-M_\\epsilon}\\Delta^{3-\\epsilon}\n                  \\prod_{i=1}^3\\left(\\sum_Tw_i(T)\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Here fixed enlargements of tubes and of the ambient region are allowed; if those enlargements are subpower on a sequence, they cost a subpower.",

        "exact_quote": "A countersequence to Proposition <a href=\"#prop:transverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:transverse\">8</a> yields a set $P$ in a fixed bounded region and three weighted tube families satisfying $$\\label{eq:transverse-point-counts}\n |P|=L^{2+o(1)},\\qquad\n |P\\cap B(z,r)|\\le L^{2+o(1)}r^2\n             \\quad(L^{-1}\\le r\\le1).$$ The points are centers of distinct cubes of side comparable to $L^{-1}$. The tubes have width $L^{-1+o(1)}$, their coefficient weights obey $\\sum_Tw_i(T)\\lesssim1$, and every cross-family direction triple has determinant at least $L^{-o(1)}$. At each $p\\in P$ there is a retained incident subfamily of total weight $e_i^*(p)=L^{-4/3+o(1)}$ such that its normalized weight law satisfies, for fixed $\\kappa>0$, $u_0>0$, $$\\label{eq:src1}\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(\\operatorname{dir}T_i\\in B(v,L^{-u})\\mid X=p\\bigr)\n       \\le L^{-\\kappa u+o(1)}\n       \\qquad(0<u<u_0,\\ u\\in\\mathbb Q).$$ This bound is uniform in $p,v$ for each fixed rational $u$. The retained subfamily may depend on $p$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-information",
        "exact_quote": "After a subsequence, all the countably many information variables used below have a common limiting joint law. For spatial, coordinate, and direction partitions with at most $L^{C+o(1)}$ possible labels, their conditional informations are uniformly integrable and have bounded limiting values. In this law the chain rule remains exact and $$\\label{eq:transverse-conditioning}\n I(Y\\mid V)\\ge I(Y\\mid V,W)\\quad\\hbox{almost surely}.$$ If, given $Z,V$, the possible values of $Y$ on an event $E_L$ number at most $L^{\\gamma+o(1)}$, include its indicator in the joint limit. On the event where the limiting indicator equals one, $I(Y\\mid Z,V)\\le\\gamma$. Alternatively, an event specified by a finite system of sampled inequalities can be treated by open-set implications with fixed slack before that slack tends to zero; no identification of arbitrary boundary events is implicit. Consequently variables determining one another up to $L^{o(1)}$ possibilities have identical limiting information, also after additional conditioning. Finite systems of limiting inequalities holding with positive probability pass to finite $L$ with any fixed positive slack.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-information",

On the event where the limiting indicator equals one, $I(Y\\mid Z,V)\\le\\gamma$. Alternatively, an event specified by a finite system of sampled inequalities can be treated by open-set implications with fixed slack before that slack tends to zero; no identification of arbitrary boundary events is implicit. Consequently variables determining one another up to $L^{o(1)}$ possibilities have identical limiting information, also after additional conditioning. Finite systems of limiting inequalities holding with positive probability pass to finite $L$ with any fixed positive slack.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-profiles",
        "exact_quote": "In the limiting law set $$F(s)=I(X_s),\\qquad G_i(s)=I(X_s\\mid T_i),\\qquad\n G_i'(s)=I(X_s\\mid T_i').$$ These functions extend from rational scales to nondecreasing Lipschitz functions on $[0,1]$, vanishing at zero. Their Lipschitz constants are respectively $3$ and $1$. Almost surely, $$\\label{eq:transverse-endpoint-information}\n F(s)\\ge2s,\\qquad F(1)=2,\\qquad\n G_i(1),G_i'(1)\\ge\\tfrac23.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-080.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-costs",
        "exact_quote": "Write $J=F(s+t)-F(s)$ and $J_i=G_i(s+t)-G_i(s)$ for the chosen reference tubes. Define $$\\ell=I(X_{s+t}\\mid C)-I(X_{s+t}\\mid C,D),\\qquad\n \\ell_i=I(X_{s+t}\\mid C,T_i)-I(X_{s+t}\\mid C,T_i,D).$$ All these limiting costs are nonnegative, and $$\\label{eq:src2}\n \\sum_{i=1}^3(J_i-\\ell_i)\n \\le\\sum_{i=1}^3I(Z_i\\mid Z_{\\widehat i},C,D)\n \\le I(Z\\mid C,D)=J-\\ell.$$ For disjoint blocks or disjoint prefixes, with the same $D$, the sum of expectations of each type of cost is $O(b_*)$. The same statement holds with any one fixed other tube in place of $T_i$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-additivity",
        "exact_quote": "Almost surely, simultaneously for every choice of one of the two tubes in each family, $$F=G_1+G_2+G_3,\\qquad G_i'=G_i,\\qquad G_i(1)=\\tfrac23.$$ In any block above, including a prefix and with any extra direction bins in $D$, $$\\label{eq:src3}\n \\begin{split}\n I(Z_i\\mid C,D)&=J_i+O\\left(\\sum_k\\ell_k\\right),\\\\\n I(Z_i,Z_j\\mid C,D)&=J_i+J_j+\n                       O\\left(\\sum_k\\ell_k\\right).\n \\end{split}$$ The absolute constants in these error bounds are fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-additivity",

          "source_id": "PM-080.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-projection",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<d<1$ and $\\rho_0>0$. There are $\\varepsilon_{\\rm pr}>0$ and $\\eta_{\\rm pr}>0$ with the following property for all sufficiently small $\\Delta$. Suppose $A\\subset B(0,1)\\subset\\mathbb R^2$, with $\\mathcal N_\\Delta$ denoting covering number by radius-$\\Delta$ balls, satisfies $$\\Delta^{-2d+\\eta_{\\rm pr}}\\le\\mathcal N_\\Delta(A)\n       \\le\\Delta^{-2d-\\eta_{\\rm pr}},\\qquad\n \\mathcal N_\\Delta(A\\cap B(z,r))\n       \\le\\Delta^{-\\eta_{\\rm pr}}r^{\\rho_0}\n                                  \\mathcal N_\\Delta(A)$$ for $\\Delta\\le r\\le1$. Let $\\sigma$ be a probability on projection lines obeying $\\sigma(B(V,r))\\le\\Delta^{-\\eta_{\\rm pr}}r^{\\rho_0}$ on the same range. Some $V\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma$ then satisfies $$\\mathcal N_\\Delta(\\pi_V A')\\ge\\Delta^{-d-\\varepsilon_{\\rm pr}}\n \\quad\\hbox{for every }A'\\subset A\\hbox{ with }\n \\mathcal N_\\Delta(A')\\ge\\Delta^{\\eta_{\\rm pr}}\n                                      \\mathcal N_\\Delta(A).$$ One may decrease both the tolerance and the gain.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-projection",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<d<1$ and $\\rho_0>0$. There are $\\varepsilon_{\\rm pr}>0$ and $\\eta_{\\rm pr}>0$ with the following property for all sufficiently small $\\Delta$. Let $\\sigma$ be a probability on projection lines obeying $\\sigma(B(V,r))\\le\\Delta^{-\\eta_{\\rm pr}}r^{\\rho_0}$ on the same range. Some $V\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma$ then satisfies $$\\mathcal N_\\Delta(\\pi_V A')\\ge\\Delta^{-d-\\varepsilon_{\\rm pr}}\n \\quad\\hbox{for every }A'\\subset A\\hbox{ with }\n \\mathcal N_\\Delta(A')\\ge\\Delta^{\\eta_{\\rm pr}}\n                                      \\mathcal N_\\Delta(A).$$ One may decrease both the tolerance and the gain.",

      "conclusion": {

        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<d<1$, $d_0>0$, and $\\kappa_0>0$. There is $\\zeta>0$, depending only on these parameters, for which the following conditions cannot hold on an event of positive limiting probability. Fix rational $[s,s+b]\\subset[0,1]$, with $b<u_0$, rational $b_*\\ge b$, and $j\\in\\{2,3\\}$. At direction precision $L^{-b_*}$ let $D_0$ be the reference triple of bins and let $D_1$ add the alternative bin. Require*\n\n1.  *$|G(s+b)-G(s)-db|\\le\\zeta b$, and $G(s+u)-G(s)\\ge d_0u-\\zeta b$;*\n\n2.  *all costs in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:transverse-costs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:transverse-costs\">13</a>, for both $D_0$ and $D_1$ and each reference tube or $T_1'$, are at most $\\zeta b$;*\n\n3.  *$h_j(u)\\ge\\kappa_0u-\\zeta b$.*\n\n*The prefix, cost, and angular requirements need be imposed only on a fixed finite rational grid of $0<u\\le b$ containing $b$, with mesh at most a sufficiently small fixed multiple of $\\zeta b$. Decreasing $\\zeta$ preserves the conclusion. All power-error constants are independent of $s,b,b_*$; the meshes can be fixed in relative coordinates $u/b$, and their finite cardinalities only affect fixed constants in the later cost sums.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-hills",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be Lipschitz with derivative $\\mathbf1_E$, and let $a<b$ be respectively a density point of $E$ and of its complement. There is a positive number $b_{\\mathrm{hill}}$ such that, for every $0<r<b_{\\mathrm{hill}}$, some $[x,y]\\subset(a,b)$ has $$r\\le y-x\\le2r,\\qquad\n G(y)-G(x)=\\tfrac12(y-x),\\qquad\n G(x+u)-G(x)\\ge\\tfrac12u\\quad(0\\le u\\le y-x).$$ For any fixed finite collection of disjoint ordered density pairs, these intervals can be chosen disjointly at every sufficiently small common $r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-finite-jumps",
        "exact_quote": "Almost surely $E$ agrees modulo null sets with a finite union of intervals and has an interior transition from an interval of full measure to an interval of zero measure. For every fixed $\\eta>0$ there is a rational block $[s,s+b]$, $0<b<u_0$, on an event of positive probability, such that $$\\label{eq:transverse-jump-block}\n G(s+b)-G(s)=\\tfrac b2+O(\\eta b),\\qquad\n G(s+b/2)-G(s)=\\tfrac b2+O(\\eta b).$$ The absolute error constants can be fixed independently of $\\eta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-finite-jumps",

          "source_id": "PM-080.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-sparse",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $c_s>0$ with the following property. Fix $\\tau_0>0$, and let $L^{\\tau_0}\\le D\\le L$. Let $U$ be a finite packet array of the form <a href=\"#eq:src5\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src5\">[eq:src5]</a>, with coefficient vector $c$, and let $\\mu$ be a nonnegative measure such that $$\\mu(B(z,r))\\le m r^2\\quad(r\\ge1),\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mu\\subset\\{|t|\\le C L^2\\}.$$ Assume the packet sparsity condition <a href=\"#eq:src6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src6\">[eq:src6]</a>, with $M=100$, for every label with nonzero coefficient. Then $$\\label{eq:src7}\n \\sum_{q\\text{ a unit lattice cube}}\n       \\mu(q)^{2/3}\\sup_q|U|^2\n \\lesssim_{\\tau_0}\n       m^{2/3}L^{4/3}D^{-c_s}\\|c\\|_{\\ell^2}^2.$$ Only finitely many uniform profile derivative bounds, depending on $\\tau_0$, are required. Consequently the same assertion holds for any prescribed finite set of field derivatives, provided the original profiles have sufficiently many bounded derivatives. Fixed enlargements of the time range and restriction to a submeasure are allowed. The exponent $c_s$ is independent of $\\tau_0$; the implicit constant and the required differentiability need not be.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Consequently the same assertion holds for any prescribed finite set of field derivatives, provided the original profiles have sufficiently many bounded derivatives.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sparse-layers",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a bounded native time interval $|\\sigma|\\le C_{\\rm nat}$, any finite number of profile seminorms on that interval, and any prescribed finite spatial decay orders there. After requiring sufficiently many derivatives of the compact profiles, an array of atoms in <a href=\"#eq:src5\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src5\">[eq:src5]</a> is a sum-integral of exact extension arrays, indexed by $k\\in\\mathbb Z^2$ and $u\\in\\mathbb R$, times a common phase $e^{iu t/L^2}$. There are nonnegative common weights $w(k,u)$, with $$\\sum_k\\int_\\mathbb Rw(k,u)\\,du<\\infty,$$ such that division by $w(k,u)$ gives all the stipulated uniform seminorm and decay bounds. For every subarray $A$ the frequency input $v_{A,k,u}$ of its layer satisfies $$\\label{eq:sparse-layer-energy}\n \\|v_{A,k,u}\\|_2^2\n    \\le C w(k,u)^2\\sum_{\\alpha\\in A}|c_\\alpha|^2.$$ Its exact frequencies lie within $O(L^{-1})$ of $\\omega_\\alpha+k/L$ for the corresponding labels.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

There are nonnegative common weights $w(k,u)$, with $$\\sum_k\\int_\\mathbb Rw(k,u)\\,du<\\infty,$$ such that division by $w(k,u)$ gives all the stipulated uniform seminorm and decay bounds. For every subarray $A$ the frequency input $v_{A,k,u}$ of its layer satisfies $$\\label{eq:sparse-layer-energy}\n \\|v_{A,k,u}\\|_2^2\n    \\le C w(k,u)^2\\sum_{\\alpha\\in A}|c_\\alpha|^2.$$ Its exact frequencies lie within $O(L^{-1})$ of $\\omega_\\alpha+k/L$ for the corresponding labels.",
          "source_id": "PM-080.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sparse-broad",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $c_b>0$ such that, whenever $K\\le s^{c_b}$, the broad part of the functional in a ball of radius $O(s^2)$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:sparse-broad}\n \\sum_{Q\\text{ broad}}\\nu(Q)^{2/3}\\|V\\|_{L^6(Q)}^2\n       \\lesssim s^{-c_b}m_0^{2/3}s^{4/3}\\mathcal E.$$ Here $\\nu(B(z,r))\\le m_0r^2$ for $r\\ge1$, and the exact frequency data for $V$ and each $V_\\theta$ have $L^2$ norms at most $C\\mathcal E^{1/2}$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-frame",
        "exact_quote": "Fix bounded frequency and profile boxes, a bound on phase-space multiplicity, and a sufficiently large envelope exponent $P$. There is a fixed finite number of profile derivatives for which the following holds uniformly. Let $h\\geq1$ and $h^{-1/2}\\leq\\delta\\lesssim1$. For $i=1,2$, let $$V_i(x,t)=\\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal I_i}s_\\ell\n e^{i(x\\xi_\\ell-t\\xi_\\ell^2)}\n H_\\ell\\left(\\frac{x-b_\\ell-2\\xi_\\ell t}{h},\\frac{t}{h^2}\\right)$$ be a finite packet sum. The profiles are supported in the fixed box and have the stipulated uniform derivative bounds. The centers $b_\\ell$ may be arbitrary real numbers, and the pairs $(b_\\ell/h,h\\xi_\\ell)$ have bounded multiplicity in every unit phase-space ball. The frequencies of the two fields lie in intervals of length $\\delta$ with distance at least a fixed positive multiple of $\\delta$. Put $$S_i(x,t)^2=\\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal I_i}|s_\\ell|^2\n \\left(1+\\frac{|x-b_\\ell-2\\xi_\\ell t|}{h}\n             +\\frac{|t|}{h^2}\\right)^{-P},\n \\qquad H_i^*=h^3\\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal I_i}|s_\\ell|^2.$$ For $A_i,B_i>0$, write $\\alpha_i=A_i/B_i$. If $\\alpha_i\\geq C$, for a sufficiently large fixed $C$, the number of unit lattice squares containing a point where simultaneously $|V_i|\\geq A_i$ and $S_i\\leq B_i$ for $i=1,2$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:src8}\n \\#\\leq C'\\delta^{-C'}\\alpha_1^{-2}\\alpha_2^{-2}\n \\left(\\frac{H_1^*}{A_1^2}+\\frac{H_2^*}{A_2^2}\\right).$$ The constants depend only on the fixed bounds just specified. In particular, they are independent of $h$, the number of packets, and the number of scales in the proof. The loss in the separation parameter has at most a fixed polynomial power.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-frame",
          "exact_quote": "Fix bounded frequency and profile boxes, a bound on phase-space multiplicity, and a sufficiently large envelope exponent $P$. If $\\alpha_i\\geq C$, for a sufficiently large fixed $C$, the number of unit lattice squares containing a point where simultaneously $|V_i|\\geq A_i$ and $S_i\\leq B_i$ for $i=1,2$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:src8}\n \\#\\leq C'\\delta^{-C'}\\alpha_1^{-2}\\alpha_2^{-2}\n \\left(\\frac{H_1^*}{A_1^2}+\\frac{H_2^*}{A_2^2}\\right).$$ The constants depend only on the fixed bounds just specified.       "exact_statement": "Fix bounded frequency and profile boxes, a bound on phase-space multiplicity, and a sufficiently large envelope exponent $P$. The frequencies of the two fields lie in intervals of length $\\delta$ with distance at least a fixed positive multiple of $\\delta$. Put $$S_i(x,t)^2=\\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal I_i}|s_\\ell|^2\n \\left(1+\\frac{|x-b_\\ell-2\\xi_\\ell t|}{h}\n             +\\frac{|t|}{h^2}\\right)^{-P},\n \\qquad H_i^*=h^3\\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal I_i}|s_\\ell|^2.$$ For $A_i,B_i>0$, write $\\alpha_i=A_i/B_i$. If $\\alpha_i\\geq C$, for a sufficiently large fixed $C$, the number of unit lattice squares containing a point where simultaneously $|V_i|\\geq A_i$ and $S_i\\leq B_i$ for $i=1,2$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:src8}\n \\#\\leq C'\\delta^{-C'}\\alpha_1^{-2}\\alpha_2^{-2}\n \\left(\\frac{H_1^*}{A_1^2}+\\frac{H_2^*}{A_2^2}\\right).$$ The constants depend only on the fixed bounds just specified. In particular, they are independent of $h$, the number of packets, and the number of scales in the proof. The loss in the separation parameter has at most a fixed polynomial power.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-frame-initial",
        "exact_quote": "It suffices to prove <a href=\"#eq:src8\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src8\">[eq:src8]</a> for packets whose spatial frequencies are supported in caps of width $O(h^{-1})$ about their assigned centers, whose spatial profiles have sufficiently rapid polynomial decay, and whose native time supports remain bounded. After dividing a field by its envelope bound $B$, its witness level is $\\alpha=A/B$ and its envelope is at most one. Given $$\\lambda=C_*/\\alpha\\leq1,$$ deleting all packets of normalized amplitude greater than $\\lambda$ changes the field at every witness by at most $C\\lambda^{-1}$. The remaining field $g_0$ has an energy parameter $D\\lesssim H^*/B^2$ such that $\\|g_0\\|_2^2\\leq D$. At a dyadic scale $\\Delta_0\\asymp h^{-1}$, its fine cap pieces $g_{0,b}$ satisfy, for all fixed orders needed below, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\| (\\partial_x-ib)^sD_b^r g_{0,b}\\|_\\infty\n &\\leq C_{r,s}\\lambda\\Delta_0^{s+2r},\n \\label{eq:frame-initial-sup}\\\\\n \\sum_b\\|(\\partial_x-ib)^sD_b^r g_{0,b}\\|_2^2\n &\\leq C_{r,s}\\Delta_0^{2s+4r}D.\n \\label{eq:frame-initial-square}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $\\|\\mathcal L^r g_0\\|_2\\lesssim_r\n\\Delta_0^{2r}\\sqrt D$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Consider the two states from the preceding construction with $0<\\lambda_i\\leq1$. Let $Q$ be a square of side $\\Delta_k^{-1}$ and let $\\mathcal Z_Q\\subset Q$ contain at most one designated point from each unit lattice square.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-strip",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a finite two-dimensional packet array of the form <a href=\"#eq:src5\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src5\">[eq:src5]</a>, with the fixed profile and phase-space bounds stated there. Suppose all frequency labels lie within distance $C/L$ of an affine line in frequency space. Let $\\mu$ be supported in a spatial square of side $O(L^2)$ and in $|t|\\leq L^2$, and suppose $\\mu(B_r)\\leq mr^2$ for every ball with arbitrary center and every $r\\geq1$. Then, with the exponent $p_0$ of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:oned\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:oned\">36</a>, $$\\label{eq:src10}\n \\mu\\{|U|\\geq gL^{-4/3}\\}\n       \\leq C mL^4g^{-p_0}\\|c\\|_2^{p_0},\\qquad g>0.$$ The constant depends only on the fixed bounds, including the strip width constant.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-rich",
        "exact_quote": "Fix bounded velocity, domain, ball, and neighborhood constants, a richness constant $c_{\\mathrm r}>0$, an angular constant $C_{\\mathrm a}>0$, exponents $A_{\\mathrm r},A_{\\mathrm a}\\geq0$, and $\\eta>0$. There are constants $A_*,C_*,C'$ depending only on these fixed data such that the following holds whenever $X\\geq2$ and $H\\geq C_*X^{A_*}$.*\n\n*Let $\\nu$ be a nonnegative measure supported in a ball of radius $O(H)$, with $$\\nu(B(q,r))\\leq C_{\\mathrm b}r^2\\qquad(r\\geq1).$$ Let $\\mathcal T$ be a finite family of lines $$\\ell_T=\\{(b_T+2\\omega_Tt,t):t\\in\\mathbb R\\},\\qquad\n |\\omega_T|\\leq C_{\\mathrm v},$$ viewed as unit-width tubes over a fixed time interval of length $O(H)$ containing the time projection of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\nu$. Write $d_T(x,t)=|x-b_T-2\\omega_Tt|$ and $e_T=(2\\omega_T,1)/|(2\\omega_T,1)|$. Assume:*\n\n\n1.  *Every tube is rich against $\\nu$: $$\\label{eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis}\n     \\int (1+d_T(q))^{-M}\\,d\\nu(q)\n     \\geq c_{\\mathrm r} H X^{-A_{\\mathrm r}},\\qquad M=100.$$*\n\n\n2.  *At $\\nu$-almost every $q$ there is a measurably chosen probability $(p_T(q))_{T\\in\\mathcal T}$, supported on tubes with $d_T(q)\\leq C_{\\mathrm n}$, such that every spherical direction ball satisfies $$\\label{eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis}\n     \\sum_{e_T\\in B_{\\mathbb S^2}(e,r)}p_T(q)\n     \\leq C_{\\mathrm a}X^{A_{\\mathrm a}}r^\\eta\n     \\qquad(r\\geq H^{-1}).$$*\n\n\n*Then each tube can be assigned to one of at most $X^{C'}$ plates (planes thickened by $X^{C'}$). Its assigned plate contains its central line segment over the relevant time interval. If $n$ is a unit normal to that plate, then $$\\label{eq:src11}\n |n\\cdot(2\\omega_T,1)|\\leq X^{C'}/H.$$ The constants are independent of the number of tubes. In particular, the richness in <a href=\"#eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis\">[eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis]</a> and the pointwise law in <a href=\"#eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis\">[eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis]</a> use exactly the same measure $\\nu$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rich",

Its assigned plate contains its central line segment over the relevant time interval. In particular, the richness in <a href=\"#eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis\">[eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis]</a> and the pointwise law in <a href=\"#eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis\">[eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis]</a> use exactly the same measure $\\nu$.",

In particular, the richness in <a href=\"#eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis\">[eq:geometry-rich-hypothesis]</a> and the pointwise law in <a href=\"#eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis\">[eq:geometry-angular-hypothesis]</a> use exactly the same measure $\\nu$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-geometry-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H\\geq1$ and let $\\sigma$ be a finite measure on $\\mathbb R^2$ of mass $m$, satisfying $$\\sigma(B(z,r))\\leq A r^{1+\\eta}\n \\qquad(z\\in\\mathbb R^2,\\ r\\geq H^{-1}),\\qquad A>0.$$ Set $\\gamma=\\eta/(1+\\eta)$. Then $$\\label{eq:geometry-parameter-energy}\n \\iint \\min\\{H,|z-z'|^{-1}\\}\\,d\\sigma(z')\\,d\\sigma(z)\n \\leq C_\\eta A^{1/(1+\\eta)}m^{1+\\gamma},$$ where the kernel on the diagonal is $H$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-geometry-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H\\geq1$ and let $\\sigma$ be a finite measure on $\\mathbb R^2$ of mass $m$, satisfying $$\\sigma(B(z,r))\\leq A r^{1+\\eta}\n \\qquad(z\\in\\mathbb R^2,\\ r\\geq H^{-1}),\\qquad A>0.$$ Set $\\gamma=\\eta/(1+\\eta)$. Then $$\\label{eq:geometry-parameter-energy}\n \\iint \\min\\{H,|z-z'|^{-1}\\}\\,d\\sigma(z')\\,d\\sigma(z)\n \\leq C_\\eta A^{1/(1+\\eta)}m^{1+\\gamma},$$ where the kernel on the diagonal is $H$.",
          "source_id": "PM-080.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $H\\geq1$ and let $\\sigma$ be a finite measure on $\\mathbb R^2$ of mass $m$, satisfying $$\\sigma(B(z,r))\\leq A r^{1+\\eta}\n \\qquad(z\\in\\mathbb R^2,\\ r\\geq H^{-1}),\\qquad A>0.$$ Set $\\gamma=\\eta/(1+\\eta)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-distribution",
        "exact_quote": "There are fixed $p>2$ and $\\sigma>0$ with the following property. Let $U$ be a finite sum of the atoms in <a href=\"#eq:src5\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src5\">[eq:src5]</a>, of packet size $L\\geq1$, with $\\sum_\\alpha|c_\\alpha|^2\\leq1$. Suppose that $\\mu$ is supported in a spatial box of side $O(L^2)$ and in $|t|\\leq L^2$, that $\\mu(B_r)\\leq mr^2$ for every ball of radius $r\\geq1$, and that <a href=\"#eq:src6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src6\">[eq:src6]</a> holds for every label, with $1\\leq D\\leq L$. Then, for every $g>0$, $$\\label{eq:src12}\n \\mu(A)\\leq C mL^4D^{-\\sigma}g^{-p},\n \\qquad A=\\{z:|U(z)|\\geq\\lambda\\},\\qquad\n \\lambda=gL^{-4/3}.$$ The constant is uniform in $L,D,g,m$ and the size of the array. It may depend on the fixed packet class, including sufficiently many finite profile derivative bounds. For an arbitrary coefficient array, the right side is multiplied by $\\|c\\|_{\\ell^2}^p$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-distribution",
          "exact_quote": "There are fixed $p>2$ and $\\sigma>0$ with the following property. Suppose that $\\mu$ is supported in a spatial box of side $O(L^2)$ and in $|t|\\leq L^2$, that $\\mu(B_r)\\leq mr^2$ for every ball of radius $r\\geq1$, and that <a href=\"#eq:src6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src6\">[eq:src6]</a> holds for every label, with $1\\leq D\\leq L$. Then, for every $g>0$, $$\\label{eq:src12}\n \\mu(A)\\leq C mL^4D^{-\\sigma}g^{-p},\n \\qquad A=\\{z:|U(z)|\\geq\\lambda\\},\\qquad\n \\lambda=gL^{-4/3}.$$ The constant is uniform in $L,D,g,m$ and the size of the array. It may depend on the fixed packet class, including sufficiently many finite profile derivative bounds. For an arbitrary coefficient array, the right side is multiplied by $\\|c\\|_{\\ell^2}^p$.",
          "source_id": "PM-080.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are fixed $p>2$ and $\\sigma>0$ with the following property. Then, for every $g>0$, $$\\label{eq:src12}\n \\mu(A)\\leq C mL^4D^{-\\sigma}g^{-p},\n \\qquad A=\\{z:|U(z)|\\geq\\lambda\\},\\qquad\n \\lambda=gL^{-4/3}.$$ The constant is uniform in $L,D,g,m$ and the size of the array. It may depend on the fixed packet class, including sufficiently many finite profile derivative bounds. For an arbitrary coefficient array, the right side is multiplied by $\\|c\\|_{\\ell^2}^p$.",

        "exact_quote": "For any subarray in a frequency square of side comparable to $r$, where $L^{-1}\\lesssim r\\leq\\delta_0$, outer induction gives $$\\label{eq:dyadic-cap-gain}\n \\mu\\{|U_{\\mathcal C}|\\geq r^\\eta\\lambda\\}\n \\leq C_{\\rm cap}C_{\\rm ind}mL^4g^{-p}\n r^{1-p/3-p\\eta}\n \\Big(\\sum_{\\alpha\\in\\mathcal C}|c_\\alpha|^2\\Big)^{p/2}.$$ The estimate holds for any submeasure of $\\mu$, with the same upper ball parameter.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dyadic-prefix",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite coefficient array be ordered, with total energy $E$, and let each output point select a prefix ending between whole coefficients. Suppose the distribution estimate for every block, on the submeasure where that block is selected, has right side $K t^{-p}\\|c_{\\rm block}\\|_{\\ell^2}^p$, where $p>2$. The field formed from the selected prefix then satisfies the same estimate with right side $C_p Kt^{-p}E^{p/2}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-080.1"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Compact atom** ([source](#stmt-def-prelim-atoms)): Let $L\geq1$, and set $R=L^2$. An atom with label $\alpha$ is the field $$A_\alpha(x,t)=L^{-1} e^{i(x\cdot\omega_\alpha-t\|\omega_\alpha\|^2)} H_\alpha\left(\frac{x-b_\alpha-2\omega_\alpha t}{L}, \frac{t}{L^2}\right). \label{eq:src5}$$ The frequencies $\omega_\alpha$ lie in a fixed square. The labels obey a joint phase-space bound: every unit ball in $\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R^2$ contains at most a fixed number of the pairs $$\left(\frac{b_\alpha}{L},L\omega_\alpha\right).$$ The profiles $H_\alpha(y,\tau)$ are supported in a fixed bounded box, and have uniform bounds for sufficiently many derivatives. More precisely, each application requires a finite integer $J$ and fixed bounds $\\|\partial^\beta H_\alpha\\|_\infty\leq C_\beta$ for $\|\beta\|\leq J$. The integer $J$ is chosen after the fixed parameters of that application; it does not grow with $L$ or with the array cardinality. A packet field is a finite sum $\sum_\alpha c_\alpha A_\alpha$, and its coefficient energy is $\sum_\alpha\|c_\alpha\|^2$, without weights.
- **Schrödinger evolution operator** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Write $S(t)f=e^{it\Delta}f$ for the $L^2$ evolution with Fourier multiplier $e^{-it\|\xi\|^2}$.

An atom with label $\\alpha$ is the field $$A_\\alpha(x,t)=L^{-1}\n e^{i(x\\cdot\\omega_\\alpha-t|\\omega_\\alpha|^2)}\n H_\\alpha\\left(\\frac{x-b_\\alpha-2\\omega_\\alpha t}{L},\n                  \\frac{t}{L^2}\\right).\n \\label{eq:src5}$$ The frequencies $\\omega_\\alpha$ lie in a fixed square. The labels obey a joint phase-space bound: every unit ball in $\\mathbb R^2\\times\\mathbb R^2$ contains at most a fixed number of the pairs $$\\left(\\frac{b_\\alpha}{L},L\\omega_\\alpha\\right).$$ The profiles $H_\\alpha(y,\\tau)$ are supported in a fixed bounded box, and have uniform bounds for sufficiently many derivatives. More precisely, each application requires a finite integer $J$ and fixed bounds $\\|\\partial^\\beta H_\\alpha\\|_\\infty\\leq C_\\beta$ for $|\\beta|\\leq J$. The integer $J$ is chosen after the fixed parameters of that application; it does not grow with $L$ or with the array cardinality. A packet field is a finite sum $\\sum_\\alpha c_\\alpha A_\\alpha$, and its coefficient energy is $\\sum_\\alpha|c_\\alpha|^2$, without weights.",
          "source_id": "PM-080.1"
        }

The labels obey a joint phase-space bound: every unit ball in $\\mathbb R^2\\times\\mathbb R^2$ contains at most a fixed number of the pairs $$\\left(\\frac{b_\\alpha}{L},L\\omega_\\alpha\\right).$$ The profiles $H_\\alpha(y,\\tau)$ are supported in a fixed bounded box, and have uniform bounds for sufficiently many derivatives. The integer $J$ is chosen after the fixed parameters of that application; it does not grow with $L$ or with the array cardinality.
